#7541. Carrot: Medical Expert, Fertility (Contractor)

Headquarters: Remote

About Carrot:

Carrot is the leading global fertility and family care platform, built on intelligent care orchestration: the right clinical guidance, at the right moment, in the context of each member’s life. More than a thousand multinational employers, health plans, and health systems trust Carrot to support millions of members across 195 countries – from pre-pregnancy through menopause and major life moments in between. Carrot's comprehensive clinical program delivers industry-leading cost savings for plan sponsors and award-winning experiences and improved outcomes for millions of people worldwide.

Carrot is widely regarded as a defining force in healthcare innovation as a recipient of several top-tier awards, including Fast Company's 'Most Innovative Companies' and CNBC's '100 Barrier Breaking Startups'. The company is regularly cited by leading global outlets — including The Economist, Bloomberg, The Wall Street Journal, NPR, ABC News, and Harvard Business Review — as a leading voice on digital health, the future of work, and family health. Learn more at get-carrot.com.

The Opportunity

Carrot is looking for a Fertility Medical Expert (RN, NP, or PA) to join our Medical Experts team on a contract basis for approximately a 3-month engagement, working a minimum 15 hours per week in a fully remote role. In this role, you will serve as a clinical resource for members navigating fertility and family-building, providing evidence-informed information and guidance that helps people understand their options and make confident, informed choices.

Day to day, you will meet with members by video to talk through their fertility questions and goals, offering clear, practical information without diagnosing, prescribing, or directing care..

This is a chance to put your fertility experience to work in a fast-growing digital health environment, with the flexibility of a contract schedule. You will work alongside a diverse team of medical experts across fertility, pregnancy, urology, mental health, and nutrition who share a passion for continuous learning and an evidence-based approach.

 

What You’ll Own

In this role, you will:

  • Provide members with evidence-informed information and guidance on fertility topics, such as fertility evaluation and testing, treatment options, and family-building pathways
  • Conduct one-on-one virtual sessions (video) that educate and present options without diagnosing, prescribing, or directing care
  • Stay current on reproductive and fertility regulatory trends and clinical practice guidelines in the US
  • Participate in quality assurance initiatives and audits to help ensure a safe, compliant member experience that meets the highest standards
  • Collaborate with the broader Medical Experts team and cross-functional partners to deliver a consistent, high-quality member experience
  • Maintain a reliable, flexible schedule within ~15 hours per week range to meet member demand throughout the engagement

 

What Success Looks Like

In your first 30 Days, you will have:

  • Delivered consistently high-quality, well-rated fertility sessions that help members feel informed and supported
  • Stayed within Carrot’s non-diagnostic, non-directive scope of practice and met documentation and quality standards across member interactions
  • Met your agreed weekly hours and availability, communicating proactively about scheduling and capacity
  • Ramped up quickly and worked independently with minimal oversight in a confidential, remote environment
  • Built trusted working relationships with the Medical Experts team and cross-functional partners

 

About You

You likely have:

  • An active US license as a Registered Nurse (RN), Nurse Practitioner (NP), or Physician Assistant (PA)
  • 3+ years of experience providing patient care in a fertility clinic or a hospital clinic that addresses reproductive health
  • Superb listening skills and the ability to empathetically discuss fertility and family-building topics
  • The ability to work independently and to distinguish good clinical judgment from non-directive guidance
  • The ability to work from a confidential and private remote environment with reliable internet and digital resources
  • Flexibility to adapt to evolving business needs and availability for 15 per week for a 3-month engagement

 

Nice to Have ⭐

While not required, we would be especially excited if you bring:

  • Experience in third-party reproduction (e.g., gestational surrogacy, donor gametes), urology, pregnancy, or menopause

#7542. Cribl: Director, Product Management (Security)

Headquarters: Remote - United States

Join the company that’s building the telemetry infrastructure for the AI era. At Cribl, we partner with IT and Security teams at many of the world’s biggest enterprises, including half of the Fortune 100, to bridge the gap between AI ambition and infrastructure reality. We’re one of the fastest‑growing private companies and a leading player in a massive, fast‑moving market. With a global workforce, we’re remote‑first and grounded in a simple idea: software is a people business. Cribl is the place where curious, collaborative people can do their best work, grow fast, and bring their full selves to the herd.

Why You’ll Love This Role

Cribl just launched Cribl Detect, our SIEM built on the Cribl platform. Detection and response is the foundation. Your job is to figure out what comes next - and then build it.

As Director of Product Management for Security Products, you'll own the next wave of Cribl's security portfolio. You'll evaluate the most promising areas beyond Detect. You'll recommend which ones to pursue and in what order, with a clear case for build, partner, or buy. Then you'll lead what we fund, from the first customer conversations to launch and growth.

We'll be honest: the exact scope isn't fixed yet. That's the point. This role is for a product leader who wants to shape a portfolio, not inherit one - and who has the security depth to make the right calls.


As An Active Member Of Our Team, You Will…

  • Drive the strategy for the next wave of security products - market analysis, customer discovery, competitive landscape, and a clear recommendation on what to do first, second, and not at all.
  • Build the business case for each area - the customer problem, market size, why Cribl can win, build / partner / buy options, and what it takes to get there.
  • Lead execution for what we fund - define the product, write the requirements, set success metrics, and drive delivery with engineering from 0 to 1 and beyond.
  • Make it one platform - make sure new capabilities work natively with Cribl Detect (shared data model, detections, alerts, and workflows), not as a set of separate tools.
  • Spend real time with customers - CISOs, SOC managers, detection engineers, and analysts - and work with design partners to validate direction before we commit.
  • Partner with Marketing, Sales, and technology partners on positioning, packaging, launch, and field enablement.
  • Grow the team - as areas get funded, hire and lead product managers to own them.
  • Represent Cribl security externally with customers, analysts, and at industry events.
  • We are a remote-first company and work happens across many time-zones - you may be required to occasionally perform duties outside your standard working hours.


If You’ve Got It - We Want It

  • 10+ years in product management or a closely related role, with 5+ years building security products.
  • Deep understanding of security operations - SIEM, detection engineering, incident response, and how SOC teams actually work day to day.
  • Hands-on product experience in at least one of: security automation and response (SOAR), identity security (ITDR, UEBA), AI or agentic security, or threat detection and response.
  • A track record of taking a product from 0 to 1 - from problem discovery to a product customers pay for and use.
  • Portfolio-level thinking - you have evaluated new markets, built business cases, and made clear recommendations with incomplete information.
  • Leadership - experience leading or mentoring PMs, and leading through influence across engineering, marketing, and sales.
  • Technical depth to go deep with engineers on data architectures, detection logic, and AI/LLM-based systems.
  • Strong written communication - you write clear strategy docs and PRDs, and you make decisions in writing.
  • Good judgment under uncertainty - you can say "we shouldn't build this" as confidently as "we should."
  • Bonus Points
    • Earlier career experience on a security operations team (SOC, detection engineering, or incident response).
    • Experience with security data platforms, data pipelines, or security data lakes.
    • Experience with partner-led product strategy or M&A evaluation.
    • Familiarity with OCSF, KQL, and MITRE ATT&CK.
    • Experience working with security customers in both the US and Europe.
    • Experience in remote-first, distributed organizations.
    • No fear - speak your mind, share your ideas.
    • Good jokes (or better yet, bad ones).
    • A love for goats.

#7543. Anthropic: Community Engagement Manager, Data Centers (Indiana & Kentucky)

Headquarters: Remote-Friendly, United States

About Anthropic

Anthropic’s mission is to create reliable, interpretable, and steerable AI systems. We want AI to be safe and beneficial for our users and for society as a whole. Our team is a quickly growing group of committed researchers, engineers, policy experts, and business leaders working together to build beneficial AI systems.

About the role

Anthropic is building AI systems that will shape the trajectory of one of the most consequential technologies in human history. That work requires physical infrastructure at significant scale, and building that infrastructure the right way means being a real, long-term partner to the communities where we operate.

We are looking for a Community Engagement Manager in Indiana and Kentucky to join our Public Policy team and serve as Anthropic's on-the-ground presence in the communities where our data centers are sited, built, and operated. This is not a communications role or a government affairs role, though you will do elements of both. It is a relationship role: your job is to make Anthropic a trusted, visible, and valued neighbor, from the earliest stages of site selection through construction and long-term operations.

You will be the connective tissue between Anthropic's Policy and Infrastructure teams and the local officials, community organizations, economic development partners, and residents who have a stake in what we build. You will surface concerns before they escalate, translate our technical and operational plans into clear local benefits, and build the coalitions that allow us to move quickly and responsibly. Because Anthropic's infrastructure footprint is still early in its growth, you will also help write the playbook: the processes you build in your first markets are the ones we will use as we expand.

You will travel regularly within Indiana and Kentucky, up to 50% of the time, and occasionally to other Anthropic data center markets.

Key responsibilities

  • Build and maintain trusted, sustained relationships with county commissioners and councils, Kentucky fiscal courts and judge-executives, mayors and city councils, plan commissions and zoning boards, state and regional economic development organizations, community groups, civic leaders, educational institutions, and workforce partners in the Indiana and Kentucky data center markets you cover

  • Develop and execute a community engagement strategy for each of your markets, spanning site selection, pre-development, construction, and ongoing operations

  • Build external coalitions, host education and listening sessions, and design local solutions that deliver real benefits to the communities where we operate

  • Represent Anthropic in public forums, town halls, planning hearings, community events, and local media

  • Monitor community sentiment and local political dynamics, and advise internal teams on risks, opportunities, and the right pace and posture for engagement

  • Partner with the Real Estate and Infrastructure team to bring community input into how projects are planned, communicated, and delivered, not just how they are announced

  • Work with the Communications team to develop proactive, locally relevant messaging

  • Coordinate charitable giving, community investment, and economic development commitments in your markets, including managing nonprofit partner relationships

  • Partner with the Energy and Sustainability team to explain our approach to environmental stewardship and respond to questions about power, water, and land use, including how large electricity needs are served by local utilities and rural electric cooperatives

  • Build a community engagement playbook and repeatable processes that scale as Anthropic's infrastructure footprint grows

Minimum qualifications

  • Experience in community affairs, public affairs, economic development, government relations, local or state government, or a closely related field

  • Direct experience working for or engaging with county and municipal governments and a broad set of community stakeholders on behalf of large infrastructure, construction, energy, or technology projects, with a working understanding of how local politics and community dynamics actually play out

  • A skilled relationship and coalition builder who earns trust with a diverse group of stakeholders, and who maintains relationships through difficult conversations

  • The ability to represent a company credibly in public settings, including town halls, hearings, and press interviews

  • Strong written and verbal communication skills, including the ability to turn a complex operational plan into plain language that a local official or reporter can understand and respond to

  • The ability to work independently, get up to speed quickly on complex policy issues, and manage multiple concurrent engagements across different markets without losing track of any of them

  • Based in Indiana or Kentucky or willing to relocate to one of them, and prepared to travel regularly across both states

Preferred qualifications

  • 7+ years of experience in community engagement specifically on data center, utility, energy, or large-scale industrial infrastructure projects, or in local government

  • Familiarity with land use, zoning, and permitting processes at the county and municipal level in Indiana and/or Kentucky, including local tax abatement and incentive approvals

  • Experience managing corporate social responsibility programs

  • Established relationships with economic development, workforce, or civic organizations in the markets this role covers

  • Experience working with real estate, legal, communications, and policy teams at the same time

  • A genuine interest in the communities where you work, not just the projects you are managing

$265,000—$295,000 USD

Legitimate Anthropic recruiters will never ask for money, fees, or banking information before your first day. How we're different

We believe that the highest-impact AI research will be big science. At Anthropic we work as a single cohesive team on just a few large-scale research efforts. And we value impact — advancing our long-term goals of steerable, trustworthy AI — rather than work on smaller and more specific puzzles. We view AI research as an empirical science, which has as much in common with physics and biology as with traditional efforts in computer science. We're an extremely collaborative group, and we host frequent research discussions to ensure that we are pursuing the highest-impact work at any given time. As such, we greatly value communication skills.

The easiest way to understand our research directions is to read our recent research. This research continues many of the directions our team worked on prior to Anthropic, including: GPT-3, Circuit-Based Interpretability, Multimodal Neurons, Scaling Laws, AI & Compute, Concrete Problems in AI Safety, and Learning from Human Preferences.

#7544. Anthropic: Anthropic STEM Fellow

Headquarters: Remote-Friendly, United States; San Francisco, CA

About Anthropic

Anthropic’s mission is to create reliable, interpretable, and steerable AI systems. We want AI to be safe and beneficial for our users and for society as a whole. Our team is a quickly growing group of committed researchers, engineers, policy experts, and business leaders working together to build beneficial AI systems.

Program Overview

Some of our world's grand challenges, from resolving long-open problems in mathematics to designing medicines for neglected diseases, require deep expert judgment that today's AI still lacks. We're working toward an AI scientist, a system capable of the long-horizon reasoning and experimental judgment needed to push the frontier on problems like these. We believe frontier AI, developed safely, can meaningfully accelerate progress across science and engineering. Getting there requires close collaboration with people who have spent years at the frontier of their fields.

The Anthropic STEM Fellows Program brings researchers and experts from across science and engineering into Anthropic for four months to take on the grand challenges of their fields with Claude. Working in small pods of Fellows from related fields, you will pair deep domain expertise with our frontier models and internal tooling to define the hardest open problems in your discipline, build the datasets and simulations that let us test and train Claude on them, and find exactly where it falls short.

We're looking for Fellows across science and engineering. Prior machine learning experience is helpful but not required. Scientific judgment and a willingness to learn quickly matter more.

Our first cohort of more than 20 STEM Fellows across mathematics, physics, the life sciences, and engineering, ran from June to September 2026. The problems they built now feed Claude's training and one of our internal science evaluations.

What to Expect:

  • A place in a small pod of Fellows working on related problems in your domain. Your pod meets weekly with Anthropic researchers to share methods, pressure-test ideas, and build on each other's work.
  • A grand challenge at the frontier of your field: an ambitious, open-ended problem scoped with our research team so you can make real progress in four months.
  • Access to frontier Claude models and internal evaluation tooling.

Compensation: 

$100,000–$167,000 USD for the four-month fellowship (equivalent to $300,000–$500,000 USD annualized), and other benefits.

Key responsibilities

  • Setting grand challenges for your field: identifying long-horizon open problems where Claude falls short of expert judgment, and defining what real progress on them looks like.
  • Building comprehensive datasets, simulations and rigorous, hard-to-game evaluations that test whether models can plan experiments, interpret data and reason about mechanisms, and systematically finding where they're confidently wrong.
  • Applying Claude to open problems in your domain, using test-time compute strategies and integrating the scientific tools and software your field relies on.

Minimum qualifications

  • Are excited about the potential for AI to reason well about hard questions in their field where expert judgment matters.
  • Hands-on experience using Claude and Claude Code in your day-to-day workflow.
  • Hold a PhD in a STEM field and have several years of experience (eg post-doc or faculty).
  • Are comfortable working in a fast-paced, high-agency atmosphere for a few months and delivering concrete results.
  • Existing authorization to work in the United States for the duration of the program.

Logistics - Fellows

  • The program runs from January 12th to May 8th 2027, and is full-time for the duration; some fellowships may be considered for an extension. 
  • Applications are open until October 28, 2026, and we plan to make offers by November 20th. Later stages will typically include a take-home exercise and conversations with our research team about your research interests and possible project directions.
  • We ask that all Fellows attend a one-week in-person orientation in San Francisco.
  • The default location is in San Francisco. We will consider remote engagements within the United States in certain circumstances.
  • We are not currently able to sponsor visas for fellows. To participate in the Fellows program, you need to have or independently obtain full-time work authorization in the United States.
  • This is a fixed-term contract position. Fellows are engaged through an employer of record, rather than employed directly by Anthropic.

#7545. Jumio: Customer Success Manager, EMEA

Headquarters: Austria (remote); UK (remote)

Role Purpose: 

The Senior Customer Success Manager will own a portfolio of enterprise and mid-market accounts across EMEA. This is not a relationship management role — it is a value consulting role. You will bring deep expertise in fraud, identity, and compliance to help customers design optimal identity strategies and realize measurable risk reduction outcomes. You will be expected to understand your customers’ business deeply, make the value Jumio delivers visible and quantifiable, and act as the connective tissue between the value that was promised during the sale and the value the customer achieves.

You will work closely with Account Managers on renewal and expansion, with the implementation team during the critical customer onboarding and adoption stages, and with Product and Support to close the loop on customer feedback. You will be a named owner of customer health — accountable for knowing which accounts are at risk, why, and what you are doing about it.

This role is well-suited to someone who is commercially-minded, data-fluent, and energized by the complexity of enterprise customer relationships. You will be joining at a moment of intentional transformation — there is a significant opportunity to shape how Jumio’s customer success motion operates going forward.

Responsibilities:

Customer Health & Retention

  • Own the health of a portfolio of EMEA enterprise and mid-market accounts — you are accountable for knowing which are green, yellow, or red and why
  • Monitor customer health signals continuously: product utilization, support ticket trends, NPS, executive engagement, and contract consumption pace
  • Identify early warning signs of dissatisfaction or disengagement before they surface as churn risk — act on health score drops immediately, don’t wait for the customer to raise the issue
  • Own escalation when product gaps or recurring issues are impacting customer outcomes — and close the feedback loop by communicating back to the customer what Jumio is doing
  • Ensure open support issues are resolved to the customer’s satisfaction and that unresolved issues never reach a renewal conversation

Fraud & Compliance Advisory

  • Serve as a subject matter expert on fraud, identity verification, AML, and digital risk management for your customer portfolio
  • Advise customer risk and fraud teams on emerging fraud vectors, industry best practices, and regulatory developments relevant to their business
  • Evaluate customer risk profiles and provide expert recommendations on verification workflow optimization, risk mitigation, and user friction tradeoffs
  • Identify compliance and regulatory hurdles that may impact product adoption and coordinate with internal teams to resolve them.

Value Realization & ROI

  • Confirm customers are seeing the outcomes they expected — pass rates, conversion improvement, fraud reduction, compliance — and make that value visible and quantifiable
  • Own the business outcomes scorecard for each account: track the metrics that matter to the customer and report on them proactively
  • Prepare and lead regular business reviews (EBRs) that drive decisions — not just report on usage
  • Connect Jumio’s performance back to the customer’s original business goals and make the ROI case clearly before the AM enters a commercial conversation
  • Address gaps between what was promised during the sale and what the customer is experiencing — proactively, not reactive

Adoption & Platform Success

  • Monitor feature adoption — ensure customers are using everything they paid for and that underutilized capabilities are proactively introduced
  • Train new users as the customer’s team grows or changes, and keep the customer current on new features and product updates relevant to their use case
  • Build a joint success plan aligned to the customer’s evolving business goals — a living document, not a one-time exercise
  • Partner with the cross-functional teams during the post-go-live period to ensure customers reach full adoption

Relationships & Executive Engagement

  • Build and maintain multi-threaded relationships across the customer organization — not just the day-to-day contact
  • Expand relationships to business leaders and executives: a single-threaded account is a renewal risk
  • Identify and nurture champions within each account who will advocate for Jumio internally
  • Invite engaged customers into the Jumio community — user groups, advisory boards, reference programs

Expansion & Commercial Partnership

  • Spot signals that a customer is ready for more — new use cases, new geographies, increased volume, new compliance requirements
  • Surface expansion opportunities to the Account Manager with a clear business case grounded in the customer’s current deployment and proven value
  • Support the AM in building the commercial case for expansion — you have the customer relationship and the data; the AM has the commercial motion
  • Identify satisfied customers who could become references, case studies, or advisory board members

Skills, Experience and Qualifications:

  • Multiple years of practitioner experience in Fraud, Identity, AML, or Digital Risk Management — you have worked inside a fraud or compliance function, not just sold to one
  • Strong consultative and analytical thinking with a focus on risk strategy and workflow design
  • Ability to distill complex fraud and compliance concepts into clear, value-driven narratives for executive stakeholders
  • Proven track record managing enterprise accounts with $500K+ ARR — you have owned renewal conversations and have GRR/NRR data to support your candidacy
  • Demonstrated ability to conduct executive-level business reviews that drive outcomes, not just report activity
  • Experience building and maintaining customer health frameworks and success plans
  • Commercial acumen: you understand the connection between customer health and revenue, and you can have credible conversations with economic buyers
  • Data fluency: you are comfortable working with product usage data, building ROI narratives, and using a CS platform (Planhat, Gainsight, Totango, or equivalent)
  • Strong communication and executive presence — you are as comfortable presenting to the C-Suite as you are troubleshooting with a technical team
    Proactive by nature: you identify risks before customers do and you act without waiting to be asked

Preferred Qualifications:

  • Familiarity with API-based or technically complex SaaS products — you do not need to be an engineer, but you need to be comfortable in technical conversations
  • Experience working in a PE-backed or high-growth transformation environment
  • Prior experience building or contributing to CS playbooks, health score models, or QBR frameworks

What Success Looks Like:

In your first 90 days:

  • You have a clear health rating for every account in your portfolio and a plan for every yellow and red account
  • You have met the key stakeholders at each account and established a regular operating cadence
  • You understand the business outcomes each customer is trying to achieve and whether Jumio is delivering them
  • You have identified the top 3 accounts at renewal risk and have an active plan in place

By end of year one:

  • GRR across your portfolio is at or above target
  • Every account has a current success plan, a health score, and an EBR cadence
  • You have surfaced expansion opportunities to the AM team with qualified business cases
  • Customer satisfaction scores across your portfolio are trending upward

Compensation:

We offer an annual full-time salary (38.5 hr week) of min 80,000 EUR gross, with a possible overpayment depending on qualifications and experience.

Jumio Values:

IDEAL: Integrity, Diversity, Empowerment, Accountability, Leading Innovation

Equal Opportunities:

Jumio is a collaboration of people with different ideas, strengths, interests and cultures. We welcome applications and colleagues from all backgrounds and of all statuses.

About Jumio:

Jumio is a B2B technology company dedicated to eradicating online identity fraud, money laundering and other financial crimes to help make the internet safer. We leverage AI, biometrics, machine learning, liveness detection and automation to create solutions that are trusted by leading brands worldwide and respected by industry thought leaders. 

Jumio is the leading provider of online identity verification, eKYC and AML solutions. With a global footprint, we’re expanding the team to meet strong client demand across a range of industries including Financial Services, Travel, Sharing Economy, Fintech, Gaming, and others.

Applicant Data Privacy

#7546. Gusto, Inc.: Senior Product Design Manager, Payroll

Headquarters: Denver, CO - Hybrid; New York, NY - Hybrid; San Francisco, CA - Hybrid; Toronto, Ontario - Remote

 

About Gusto

At Gusto, we're on a mission to grow the small business economy. We handle the hard stuff — payroll, health insurance, 401(k)s, and HR — so owners can focus on their craft and their customers. With teams in Denver, San Francisco, and New York, we support more than 500,000 small businesses nationwide and are building a workplace that reflects the people we serve.

All full-time employees receive competitive base pay, benefits, and equity (RSUs) — because everyone who helps build Gusto should share in its success. Offer amounts are determined by role, level, and location. Learn more about our Total Rewards philosophy.

AI is a fundamental part of how work gets done at Gusto. We expect all team members to actively engage with AI tools relevant to their role and grow their fluency as the technology evolves. AI experience requirements vary by role and will be assessed during the interview process.

About Design at Gusto:

Design at Gusto is made up of over 80 creative, collaborative people who care deeply about our mission to empower small businesses and their employees. We're a cross-functional group that is always looking for opportunities to build understanding and empathy for the people who use Gusto. We don't care much about swim lanes and though we care deeply about quality and craft, we are never precious about it. We work closely with cross-functional partners in Product, Engineering, Data, and Marketing to design, build, and ship experiences that make a real difference. We believe great design can make hard things possible, even delightful.

We are building an AI-native design organization — and we mean that seriously. We're looking for designers who are already working this way: using AI to prototype ideas quickly, shorten customer feedback loops, and ship quality UX directly to customers. This isn't a future aspiration — it's how we work now, and it's changing what the design role looks like at Gusto and across the industry.

About the Role:
For the hundreds of thousands of small businesses we serve, our mission starts with one of their most critical responsibilities: paying their team. Getting payroll right isn’t just a feature—it’s the foundation of trust with every business that relies on us.
As the Product Design Manager for Payroll, you'll lead design in this senior level position for one of Gusto's most complex and consequential product areas, shaping the future of a product that millions of people rely on for financial stability and personal prosperity. This is a high-impact, high-visibility role with broad scope. You'll manage a team of designers and set the UX direction for Payroll, partnering closely with Product, Engineering, Research, Data, Ops, Compliance, and others to take on payroll complexity so our customers don't have to.
 
Your work will include simplifying complex, reciprocal workflows, defining a long-term UX vision and rallying others around it, and leading automation and AI initiatives that make the work of paying a team disappear. You'll also mentor designers, set the quality and judgment standard for AI-assisted design work across the broader Design team, and contribute to platform standards that help others across Gusto build consistent, high-quality experiences.
 
About the Team:
Payroll is the foundation of Gusto and our most-used, most-loved product. It’s the first Gusto experience many customers interact with, and it continues to power their success as they grow. Payroll also underpins our broader ecosystem, enabling the adoption of Benefits, Time Tracking, HR tools, and so much more. Our team is tasked with evolving and scaling this core experience for small businesses of all sizes, from solo entrepreneurs to growing companies. We focus on taking on the complexity of payroll so our customers don’t have to—making payroll faster, more flexible, and increasingly hands off.
 
Here’s what you’ll do day-to-day:
  • Lead end-to-end design for Payroll — shaping experiences that help small businesses pay their teams correctly, on time, and with confidence.
  • Define and communicate the long-term UX vision for Payroll, and rally multiple teams around it.
  • Partner with Product, Engineering, Data, Ops and others to set strategy and roadmaps.
  • Lead product initiatives where AI and automation are central to the user experience, simplifying payroll and reducing manual work without compromising accuracy or trust.
  • Collaborate with Research and Data to uncover customer insights and translate them into clear, actionable UX priorities.
  • Influence product and technical roadmaps by advocating for design quality, customer needs, and long-term system health.
  • Set the quality and judgment standard for all design work — whether AI-assisted or not — including when to push further and when outputs meet the bar.
  • Define and scale shared patterns, principles, and standards that promote quality, consistency, and velocity.
  • Manage and grow a team of designers, providing mentorship, clear direction, and hands-on support across the team.
  • Champion the Payroll experience as the foundation of Gusto's broader People Platform, ensuring seamless integration across Benefits, Time & Attendance, and third-party tools.
Here’s what we're looking for:
  • 8–10+ years of product design experience, with at least 3 years leading and managing design teams.
  • Experience designing in complex domains i.e. financial products, healthcare, or similarly regulated environments.
  • Experience setting UX direction across multiple teams and influencing product strategy — not just executing on it.
  • Proven ability to drive alignment across cross-functional partners and represent design in high-stakes, strategic conversations.
  • Deep systems thinking, with a track record of turning interconnected, complex workflows into experiences that feel simple and intuitive.
  • Clear, direct communication and storytelling skills, with the ability to explain design decisions and influence partners at all levels.
  • A genuine bond to Gusto's mission and the needs of small businesses.

Ideally you have:

  • Demonstrated ability to assess AI-generated design directions: knowing when to use them, when to override them, and how to raise the quality bar on AI outputs.
  • Experience leading product initiatives where AI or automation is central to the user experience — including how to represent uncertainty, surface errors gracefully, and preserve user agency when automation gets it wrong, particularly in high-stakes contexts.
  • Experience leading a team through an AI workflow transition — not just adopting AI personally, but changing how a team works, including establishing new norms and standards.

Our cash compensation range for this role is $172,000/yr to $237,000/yr in Denver & most remote locations, and $202,000/yr to $279,000/yr in San Francisco, Seattle & New York. Final offer amounts are determined by multiple factors, including candidate experience and expertise, and may vary from the amounts listed above.

At Gusto, we don't use titles the traditional way, as a signal of rank or hierarchy. Instead, our titles describe two things at once — your craft (what you do, e.g. Engineering, Design) and your scope of ownership (what you're accountable for) — which is why you'll see something like "Head of X" instead of a rank word like "Director" that tells you neither. Because of this, the title on this posting may look different from what you'd expect based on the scope and seniority of the role, and from how similar roles are typically titled elsewhere. If you have questions about how this role compares to your current title or level, your recruiter can help translate.

Note: The San Francisco office expectations encompass both the San Francisco and San Jose metro areas. 

When approved to work from a location other than a Gusto office, a secure, reliable, and consistent internet connection is required. This includes non-office days for hybrid employees.

Reliable, consistent presence: We expect regular and predictable attendance, whether you're working in-office, hybrid, or fully remote, so that you stay closely connected to your team, customers, and stakeholders in real time and ensure colleagues and customers receive the timely support and responsiveness they deserve.

Our customers come from all walks of life and so do we. We hire great people from a wide variety of backgrounds, not just because it's the right thing to do, but because it makes our company stronger. If you share our values and our enthusiasm for small businesses, you will find a home at Gusto.

#7547. StockX: Category Manager - Trading Cards

Headquarters: Remote, USA

Help empower our global customers to connect to culture through their passions.

Why you’ll love this role:

StockX is the world’s leading marketplace for current culture, built at the intersection of commerce, community, and credibility. Trading Cards are foundational to StockX — not just as products, but as artifacts of fandom, nostalgia, speculation, and identity.

We are expanding how Trading Cards culture shows up on StockX through creator-driven, community-oriented commerce experiences that blend storytelling, expertise, and transaction. To do this well, we need category leaders who don’t just understand collectibles — they live inside them.

The Trading Cards Category Manager is the single-threaded owner of collectibles category growth within a new, high-priority commerce initiative. This person is the in-house expert on trading cards and sports cards, — and is responsible for translating that expertise into supply growth, engagement, and GMV.

You will act as:

  • The internal authority on the first-hand and second-hand trading cards ecosystem
  • The external face of StockX to collectors, breakers, shops, graders, dealers, and community leaders
  • The architect of the trading cards go-to-market playbook for this new initiative

This role blends deep cultural fluency, strategic ownership, and hands-on execution. Early on, you will own everything related to trading cards within this initiative. As the category scales, you will grow into a leader overseeing a broader trading cards portfolio and team.

What You’ll Do:

Own the Collectibles Category End-to-End

  • Define and execute the growth strategy for trading cards within the platform
  • Own category-level performance across supply, engagement, and revenue
  • Establish quarterly goals, leading indicators, and operating rhythms

Be the Market & Culture Expert

Maintain deep, current knowledge of trading card culture, including:

  • Key sellers, breakers, dealers, shops, graders, creators, and brands
  • Sub-segments such as trading cards (sports and TCG), graded sports cards, etc.
  • Product drops, set releases, grading cycles, convention moments, and cultural spikes
  • Pricing dynamics, liquidity, population reports, grading premiums, and resale economics

Serve as the internal voice of the trading cards community, informing product, marketing, and platform decisions.

Supply & Creator Acquisition

  • Build and own a pipeline of high-impact sellers across trading cards, sports cards, etc.
  • Personally recruit and onboard flagship sellers, breakers, comic shops, etc. who set the tone for the platform
  • Develop seller strategies that balance credibility, storytelling, and commerce

Commerce Strategy & Category Moments

  • Identify and orchestrate trading card-specific moments, campaigns, and formats that drive engagement and conversion (e.g., major set releases, championship spikes, grading returns, convention cycles)
  • Partner with Category Marketing to design culturally authentic activations
  • Experiment with new approaches (live breaks, expert drops, themed auctions, etc.) and iterate quickly based on performance and feedback

Cross-Functional Leadership

  • Work closely with Account and Partnerships Managers to scale seller acquisition and support
  • Collaborate with Product and Engineering to shape features and tools that matter to collectors (e.g., condition transparency, grading integrations, pricing insights, population data visibility)
  • Provide clear, actionable insights from the market to influence roadmap prioritization

Build the Playbook

  • Document learnings, frameworks, and best practices that can be reused across future categories
  • Help define what “great” looks like for category ownership at StockX
  • Support hiring and onboarding as the trading card category team grows

About You:

  • Deeply embedded in trading card culture — you know the people, platforms, trends, grading standards, and economics, and you’re already plugged into the ecosystem
  • 5+ years of experience in category management, partnerships, marketplace growth, brand, dealer, or operator roles within trading cards, sports cards, comics, toys, coins, or adjacent enthusiast ecosystems
  • Comfortable owning outcomes, not just strategy — you execute, iterate, and adapt
  • Strong relationship builder with credibility among collectors, breakers, shop owners, graders, dealers, and community members
  • Data-literate and commercially minded — you understand pricing spreads, grading premiums, liquidity, margin structures, and incentives
  • Entrepreneurial, scrappy, and energized by building something from the ground up

Nice to Have:

  • Existing network of card breakers, LCS owners, comic shops, grading submitters, toy dealers, or coin specialists
  • Experience buying, selling, grading, or operating within collectibles marketplaces
  • Familiarity with grading ecosystems (e.g., card and comic grading workflows, condition standards, population reports)
  • Experience with content-driven commerce, live selling, or enthusiast communities
  • Experience working cross-functionally with product and engineering teams
  • Experience running your own collectibles business, shop, or online storefront

Why This Role Matters:

Trading Cards will define the early perception and success of this new commerce initiative at StockX. This role sets:

  • The cultural and credibility bar for the platform
  • The go-to-market blueprint for future enthusiast categories
  • StockX’s reputation within trading cards

You will have outsized influence on how StockX expands its leadership from marketplace into deeper cultural engagement within the global trading card ecosystem.

About StockX

StockX is proud to be a Detroit-based technology leader focused on the large and growing online market for sneakers, apparel, accessories, electronics, collectibles, trading cards, and more. StockX's powerful platform connects buyers and sellers of high-demand consumer goods from around the world using dynamic pricing mechanics. This approach affords access and market visibility powered by real-time data that empowers buyers and sellers to determine and transact based on market value. The StockX platform features hundreds of brands across verticals including Jordan Brand, adidas, Nike, Supreme, BAPE, Off-White, Louis Vuitton, Gucci; collectibles from brands including LEGO, KAWS, Bearbrick, and Pop Mart; and electronics from industry-leading manufacturers Sony, Microsoft, Meta, and Apple. Launched in 2016, StockX employs 1,000 people across offices and verification centers around the world. Learn more at www.stockx.com.

The Opportunity

Carrot is seeking a Lead, Product Management to help shape and scale one of our core product areas as Carrot's platform continues to grow. In this role, you'll operate as a strategic driver within the Product organization — owning a significant portion of the product roadmap, setting direction for cross-functional initiatives, and raising the bar for product craft across the team.

This is a highly visible, high-impact role for someone who has already demonstrated strong product judgment and is ready to take on greater scope: shaping strategy, mentoring other product managers, and partnering directly with senior leadership on the initiatives that matter most to our members and business.

What You’ll Own

In this role, you will:

  • Own the end-to-end product strategy and roadmap for a key product area, translating company priorities into a clear, prioritized plan of execution
  • Lead cross-functional initiatives, aligning Engineering, UX, Clinical, Legal, and Commercial partners around a shared vision and set of goals
  • Serve as a thought partner and informal mentor to other Product Managers, helping raise the bar on product process, discovery, and craft across the team
  • Make and communicate high-judgment prioritization decisions, balancing member experience, business impact, and technical feasibility
  • Partner directly with senior product and cross-functional leadership to shape quarterly and annual product strategy
  • Identify and address systemic gaps in process, tooling, or team practices that limit the Product team's effectiveness
  • Represent the Product team in executive and cross-functional forums, clearly communicating strategy, trade-offs, and outcomes

 

What Success Looks Like

  • Delivered a body of work that measurably improved member outcomes, engagement, or business performance within your product area
  • Built trusted, effective working relationships with cross-functional leaders across Engineering, UX, Clinical, Legal, and Commercial/Customer Success
  • Established yourself as a go-to product thought leader, with other PMs seeking out your input on strategy and process
  • Shaped or improved a core product process (discovery, roadmapping, prioritization) that is now used more broadly across the team
  • Successfully led at least one significant, ambiguous, cross-functional initiative from concept through launch

 

About You 

You likely have:

  • 5+ years of product management experience, including time at a fast-growing technology company; healthcare or digital health experience is a plus
  • A track record of owning complex, cross-functional product initiatives from strategy through execution
  • Strong product sense and the ability to balance user needs, business goals, and technical constraints in your decision-making
  • Excellent written and verbal communication skills, with the ability to influence and align both peers and senior leadership
  • Experience mentoring or informally guiding less-experienced product managers

 

Nice to Have ⭐

  • Experience in fertility, family-building, or broader digital health benefits
  • Experience partnering with Clinical or Legal teams on regulated or compliance-sensitive products
  • Familiarity with AI-enabled product features or workflows
  • Experience operating in a fully distributed, remote-first organization

 

Why This Role Matters 

As Carrot continues to scale, our Product team needs leaders who can operate with both strategic clarity and hands-on execution. This Lead, Product Management role is central to that effort — helping ensure our roadmap reflects the right priorities, our cross-functional partners are aligned, and our product practices scale sustainably as the team grows.

This is an opportunity to have an outsized impact: shaping not just a single product area, but the way Carrot's Product organization operates as it matures.

#7551. Gusto, Inc.: Payment Operations Analyst

Headquarters: San Francisco, CA - Hybrid; Seattle, WA - Remote

 

About Gusto

At Gusto, we're on a mission to grow the small business economy. We handle the hard stuff — payroll, health insurance, 401(k)s, and HR — so owners can focus on their craft and their customers. With teams in Denver, San Francisco, and New York, we support more than 500,000 small businesses nationwide and are building a workplace that reflects the people we serve.

All full-time employees receive competitive base pay, benefits, and equity (RSUs) — because everyone who helps build Gusto should share in its success. Offer amounts are determined by role, level, and location. Learn more about our Total Rewards philosophy.

AI is a fundamental part of how work gets done at Gusto. We expect all team members to actively engage with AI tools relevant to their role and grow their fluency as the technology evolves. AI experience requirements vary by role and will be assessed during the interview process.

About the Role:

Payments at Gusto aren't just infrastructure — they're the reason people get paid on time, every time. Payment Operations sits within Gusto's Money team, a cross-functional group spanning Engineering, Product, and Operations that powers money movement across Gusto's growing product surface: core payroll, contractor payments, international money movement, and new business offerings like PEO.

As a Payment Operations Analyst, you'll own specific end-to-end payment workflows, resolve exceptions that require real judgment, and help make our systems more reliable and efficient over time. Gusto's payment surface area is expanding — and this team is at the center of that growth.

This is a role for someone who can shift from investigating a single misfired transaction to coordinating a response on a payment outage affecting thousands — and bring the same rigor to both. You think in systems. You hold partners accountable. You lead with curiosity and embrace AI as a core part of how you work.

You'll collaborate closely with Engineering, Product, Accounting, Risk, and CX — and you'll develop strong day-to-day relationships with Gusto's bank and payment partners. When something breaks or slows down, you're the one who drives it to resolution.

 

Here’s what you’ll do day-to-day:

Support the payment lifecycle end-to-end

  • Own the operational layer of specific payment workflows — ACH processing, exception handling, refunds, collection re-debits, reconciliation. When something is off, you investigate, escalate where needed, and see it through to resolution. And when you see a pattern, you push to fix the root cause.

Build alongside Product and Engineering

  • Payment Operations doesn't just react — we shape how the systems work. You'll contribute to product specs, surface operational pain points, help define requirements, and participate in launches. You're a partner in building, not just a consumer of what gets shipped.

Close the loop on customer pain

  • Customer and CX feedback is signal. You actively mine it to identify friction in payment flows, prioritize what matters, and bring those insights into the operational and product conversations where decisions get made.

Think in systems

  • Connect the dots across rails, partners, reconciliation, compliance, and customer experience. When an issue surfaces, you understand how it ripples — and you bring that perspective to cross-functional conversations.

Hold partners to a high standard

  • Work directly with Gusto's bank and payment partners — domestic and international — to triage issues, resolve escalations, and maintain strong working relationships. You know how to be firm and collaborative at the same time, and you don't let things sit.
    Lead with AI
    You use AI as a genuine work partner — to move faster on analysis, draft communications, synthesize data, and automate the repetitive. You're always looking for the smarter way to get to the answer.

Here’s what we're looking for:

  • 3+ years of experience in payment operations, banking operations, treasury operations, or a related role at a fintech or financial institution
  • Working knowledge of domestic payment rails (ACH/Nacha, wires, RTP) and/or international money movement (cross-border transfers, FX, global disbursement networks); experience across both is a strong plus
  • Strong analytical instincts — you look for the pattern behind the problem and can query or structure data to find it (SQL a plus but not required)
  • Experience working with bank portals, payment processors, or reconciliation tools
  • Ability to communicate clearly with both operational and technical counterparts; you can write a crisp Slack to an engineer and a professional escalation to a bank partner
  • Comfortable working in ambiguity and taking ownership without waiting for perfect information
  • Eager to build AI into how you work — whether you're already using it daily or just getting started, you see it as a genuine productivity lever and are committed to developing that fluency
  • Knowledge of relevant compliance frameworks (Nacha operating rules, unclaimed property, money transmission) is a strong plus

Our cash compensation amount for this role is targeted at $78,000-$97,000 /yr in Denver & most remote locations, and $97,000-$121,000 /yr for San Francisco, Seattle & New York. Final offer amounts are determined by multiple factors, including candidate experience and expertise, and may vary from the amounts listed above.

At Gusto, we don't use titles the traditional way, as a signal of rank or hierarchy. Instead, our titles describe two things at once — your craft (what you do, e.g. Engineering, Design) and your scope of ownership (what you're accountable for) — which is why you'll see something like "Head of X" instead of a rank word like "Director" that tells you neither. Because of this, the title on this posting may look different from what you'd expect based on the scope and seniority of the role, and from how similar roles are typically titled elsewhere. What you’ll do when you join us:

Product Strategy & Vision

  • Collaborate with the VP, Product and other members of the leadership team in the development of portfolio-expanding new products aligned with our diversification strategy and organizational mission
  • Translate student, educator, and district needs into a compelling, scalable product vision
  • Define success metrics and establish product goals grounded in user value, learning outcomes, and business impact
  • Own the product roadmap and backlog prioritization, and hold final accountability for stage-gate go/no-go decisions

Portfolio Governance & Board Reporting

  • Represent the product and team at quarterly Impact Committee (Board of Directors) meetings and at key project milestones, presenting business cases, evidence, and progress to build board confidence and credibility in the product line; bring core team members into these sessions when it strengthens that case
  • Participate in monthly Bridge Role meetings with department directors—the connective tissue between the product team and cross-departmental leadership—presenting roadmap, business case, and evidence updates, and partnering on planning for the product’s ongoing support and growth beyond launch

Market Research & Concept Development

  • Conduct ongoing market research to identify trends, gaps, and opportunities in the education sector.
  • Collaborate with educators, administrators, and technology partners to ensure product relevance and feasibility.
  • Generate, refine, and prioritize innovative product ideas that align with impact and revenue goals.

0–1 Product Development

  • Lead the design and development of product prototypes that bring early concepts to life and enable rapid iteration.
  • Design and execute testing strategies to gather user feedback, validate assumptions, and refine product concepts and prototypes.
  • Lead roadmap creation and iterative development from concept to launch
  • Build business cases and secure executive buy-in and resources

Execution & Agile Delivery

  • Own and continuously refine the product backlog in the team’s tools of record (e.g., Jira, Confluence), writing clear epics, stories, and acceptance criteria that translate strategy into sprint-ready work
  • Facilitate agile ceremonies—sprint planning, backlog refinement, and retrospectives—and maintain a consistent sprint cadence in partnership with delivery/project management
  • Track delivery health (velocity, cycle time) and drive iterative, evidence-based delivery: ship in small increments and adjust scope based on real user feedback and data

Cross-Functional Leadership

  • Collaborate with researchers, instructional designers, UX, engineering, and go-to-market teams, with clear ownership across the team
  • Facilitate agile rituals and ensure alignment across product, design, and technical execution
  • Manage timelines, dependencies, and deliverables to ensure high-quality outcomes

Operational & Financial Oversight

  • Manage the budget for product innovation, ensuring efficient and strategic use of resources.
  • Monitor product performance and iterate based on user feedback, learning outcomes, and business impact.

Here’s what we’re looking for:

  • 5+ years of product management experience, ideally in EdTech or youth-focused industries
  • Proven track record leading 0–1 product development and launching new products that achieved scale and revenue targets
  • Demonstrated experience using AI tools for product development, user research, or personalization
  • Hands-on experience with agile/Scrum or Kanban practices and tools (e.g., Jira, Confluence) for backlog management, sprint planning, and delivery tracking
  • Experience managing or influencing cross-functional teams
  • Deep understanding of K–12 education markets, especially district and school purchasing dynamics
  • Excellent communication and leadership skills
  • Travel Requirements: Travel is typically quarterly but may be as frequent as every two months for research, testing, and other activities as needed.

  • Maintain the Salesforce-side architecture for marketing-to-sales handoffs, campaign member data, source fields, conversion logic, and reporting quality.
  • Keep current marketing workflows stable while future marketing cloud and CDP options are evaluated.
  1. Future Data 360, SFMC Advanced, Adobe, Oracle, and CDP evaluation
  • Lead the requirements, scorecard, architecture review, vendor comparison, data governance review, and implementation readiness plan for future customer data and marketing cloud decisions.
  • Evaluate Salesforce Data 360, Salesforce Marketing Cloud Advanced, Adobe Marketing Cloud, Oracle Responsys, and related CDP needs against GTM use cases, data model, activation, consent, reporting, integration, cost, and operating complexity.
  • If a platform is selected, lead implementation planning and execution with clear phases, owners, risks, testing, migration, enablement, and adoption metrics.
  1. DealHub CPQ integration, data quality, releases, and adoption
  • Own Salesforce-side integration quality for DealHub CPQ, including quote data, approval sync, product lines, opportunity fields, order handoffs, and reporting impact.
  • Run release management with intake, solution design, testing, regression checks, deployment, communication, documentation, and adoption tracking.

Job Brief

We are expanding our focus to cover AI-augmented threat intelligence and post-quantum cryptographic (PQC) security. The ideal candidate will combine deep blockchain forensics expertise with working knowledge of machine learning techniques for anomaly detection, and awareness of quantum computing threats to current cryptographic foundations (ECDSA, RSA) used across blockchain ecosystems. We value candidates who can bridge academia and industry and build collaborative relationships with research institutions to accelerate the adoption of emerging cryptographic and AI-driven techniques within the company.
 

Main Responsibilities of a Blockchain Risk Control Analyst/Researcher

Your main responsibilities will include:
  • Leveraging LLM techniques to drive the development of next-generation on-chain risk identification and threat intelligence systems, including autonomous agent workflows capable of continuous monitoring, self-triggered investigation, and automated operational responses — reducing manual intervention across the full risk lifecycle from detection to resolution;
  • Leading forensic investigations on real crypto theft, fraud, and market manipulation cases, integrating AI-assisted pattern recognition to accelerate root cause analysis;
  • Researching post-quantum cryptographic algorithms (NIST PQC: ML-KEM, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA, FALCON) and their implications for blockchain security; assessing quantum computing threats to existing cryptographic foundations (ECDSA secp256k1, hash functions) and evaluating migration paths;
  • Partnering with internal teams and external stakeholders for incident response, and engaging with academic institutions to translate frontier research into deployable security tools;
 

Key Requirements

  • You have a Master's or PhD's degree in Blockchain security, Computer Science, Data Science, or a related field.
  • You are good at programming languages commonly used in blockchain analytics (e.g., SQL, Python, Javascript)
  • Experience applying machine learning or AI techniques to blockchain data analysis, fraud detection, or threat intelligence
  • A strong publication record in top-level industry or academic conferences is highly valued.
  • You have experience with blockchain monitoring/forensic tools and analytics platforms.
  • Familiarity with NIST PQC standards (ML-KEM, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA, FALCON) and lattice/hash-based cryptography fundamentals is a plus;
  • Understanding of quantum computing threats to elliptic curve cryptography and implications for key management in Web3;
  • You have security or risk control experience as a plus.
  • You possess effective communication and collaboration abilities.
  • English is essential, and proficiency in Mandarin can be an advantage.

#7569. Cadence Health: Bilingual/Spanish Speaking Licensed Practical Nurse

Headquarters: Remote

Cadence is the clinical AI company automating the treatment of chronic disease. Its Clinical Intelligence takes on the routine, high-volume work of chronic care – monitoring patients, surfacing risks, and coordinating action – so clinicians can focus on the decisions that require them. Grounded in partnerships with more than 20 leading health systems, deep EMR integrations, dedicated medical group, and experience serving a population of more than 100,000 patients, the system learns from real-world care delivery, improving as it scales. Cadence has been recognized by TIME as one of the Top 100 HealthTech Companies and by LinkedIn as a Top Startup (#4, 2025).

The Role

We’re hiring a Bilingual/Spanish Speaking Clinical Navigator (Licensed Practical Nurse) to deliver virtual chronic care support for Medicare patients enrolled in Cadence care programs.

Clinical Navigators deliver proactive, patient-centered outreach and follow-through. Depending on program needs, this role includes structured patient check-ins, coaching and goal-setting, care coordination to close gaps in care, and addressing barriers such as access to resources, transportation, or medication refills. Clinical Navigators document patient interactions in the Cadence platform and collaborate closely with the clinical team to escalate concerns and coordinate next steps, ensuring patients feel supported, informed, and engaged throughout their care journey.

Schedule

This is a full-time, remote position based in the United States. Standard working hours are Monday through Friday, with the following schedule options currently available:
  • Central Time: 9am to 6pm CT only
  • Mountain, Arizona, or Pacific Time: 8am to 5pm or 9am to 6pm

What You’ll Do

  • Conduct outbound telephonic patient outreach and provide one-on-one coaching and support to patients managing chronic conditions, including but not limited to type 2 diabetes, hypertension, and cardiovascular disease.
  • Help patients execute personalized care plans and achieve their goals by providing education and coaching focused on behavior modification, nutrition, physical activity, and self-management strategies using evidence-based techniques such as motivational interviewing and SMART goals.
  • Conduct comprehensive assessments of patients' health status, lifestyle behaviors, nutritional habits, and readiness to change.
  • Educate patients on disease management, medication adherence, symptom recognition, and prevention strategies, and execute care delivery tasks in accordance with Cadence clinical policies and established protocols, under appropriate clinical supervision.
  • Monitor patients' progress, adherence to treatment plans, and health outcomes through regular check-ins and remote monitoring tools.
  • Document patient interactions, outcomes, and follow-up plans in the Cadence platform; identify barriers to care (including social needs) and connect patients with resources or internal support pathways.
  • Collaborate in real time with clinicians and patient support teams using tools such as our Cadence Platform, Google Workspace, and Slack, and contribute feedback to improve workflows in a fast-moving, change-oriented environment.

These responsibilities are intended to describe the general nature and level of work being performed by personnel assigned to this job classification. They are not to be construed as an exhaustive list of job duties performed by personnel in this classification. Other job related duties may be assigned by management.

Who You Are

  • A clinician who thrives at the intersection of rigorous care standards and a care delivery model that is still being built.
  • Skilled in patient education, coaching, and care coordination - you meet patients where they are in life, considering their age, lifestyle, and diet.
  • Disciplined in documentation and follow-through, with the attention to detail required for accurate clinical records in a remote environment.
  • Reliable and consistent in schedule adherence, with the interpersonal communication skills to build trust with patients over the phone.
  • Comfortable working independently in a remote, fast-paced environment with evolving workflows, and tech-savvy enough to navigate and troubleshoot multiple systems simultaneously while keeping patients engaged.
  • Comfortable using AI-assisted clinical tools (such as Gemini or Open Evidence) to support patient interactions and surface relevant clinical context, while maintaining clinical judgment as the primary guide for every patient decision.
  • Biased toward action - you bring clinical authority to every interaction, make the call when it counts, and take ownership of outcomes.

What You Need

  • An active compact multi-state LPN/LVN license in the state where you currently reside is required.
  • At least 5 years of clinical experience as a Licensed Practical Nurse is required.
  • Remote work readiness (including stable high-speed WiFi) is required.
  • An active CA state LVN license is highly preferred.
  • Fluency in Spanish is required.
  • Experience providing patient education and support to individuals managing chronic conditions is preferred.
  • Fluency with AI-assisted clinical tools (such as Gemini, Open Evidence, or comparable platforms) and the ability to speak credibly with providers and patients about where AI supports care delivery and where human judgment remains non-negotiable.

The anticipated salary range for this role is $26 - $30 per hour, which is based on role scope, level, and location. In addition to base compensation, this role may be eligible for incentive compensation as part of the overall total rewards package. This position is remote and based in the United States. Actual compensation will be determined based on factors including experience, skills, internal equity, and applicable law.

#7573. Cadence Health: Bilingual/Spanish Speaking Health Coach

Headquarters: Remote

Cadence is the clinical AI company automating the treatment of chronic disease. Its Clinical Intelligence takes on the routine, high-volume work of chronic care – monitoring patients, surfacing risks, and coordinating action – so clinicians can focus on the decisions that require them. Grounded in partnerships with more than 20 leading health systems, deep EMR integrations, dedicated medical group, and experience serving a population of more than 100,000 patients, the system learns from real-world care delivery, improving as it scales. Cadence has been recognized by TIME as one of the Top 100 HealthTech Companies and by LinkedIn as a Top Startup (#4, 2025).

The Role

We’re hiring a Bilingual/Spanish Speaking Clinical Navigator (Health Coach) to deliver virtual chronic care support for Medicare patients enrolled in Cadence care programs.

Clinical Navigators deliver proactive, patient-centered outreach and follow-through. Depending on program needs, this role includes structured patient check-ins, coaching and goal-setting, care coordination to close gaps in care, and addressing barriers such as access to resources, transportation, or medication refills. Clinical Navigators document patient interactions in the Cadence platform and collaborate closely with the clinical team to escalate concerns and coordinate next steps, ensuring patients feel supported, informed, and engaged throughout their care journey.

Schedule

This is a full-time, remote position based in the United States. Standard working hours are Monday through Friday, with the following schedule options:

  • Mountain, Arizona, or Pacific Time: 8am to 5pm or 9am to 6pm
  • Central Time: 9am to 6pm CT only

What You’ll Do

  • Conduct outbound telephonic patient outreach and provide one-on-one coaching and support to patients managing chronic conditions, including but not limited to type 2 diabetes, hypertension, and cardiovascular disease.
  • Help patients execute personalized care plans and achieve their goals by providing education and coaching focused on behavior modification, nutrition, physical activity, and self-management strategies using evidence-based techniques such as motivational interviewing and SMART goals.
  • Conduct comprehensive assessments of patients' health status, lifestyle behaviors, nutritional habits, and readiness to change.
  • Educate patients on disease management, medication adherence, symptom recognition, and prevention strategies, and execute care delivery tasks in accordance with Cadence clinical policies and established protocols, under appropriate clinical supervision.
  • Monitor patients' progress, adherence to treatment plans, and health outcomes through regular check-ins and remote monitoring tools.
